        if !input.regions.contains_key(&p.name) {

                return Err(fail("§5.1: mem slot requires region type"));
            };
            let n = (a < 64)
                .then(|| 1usize.checked_shl(a as u32))
                .flatten()
                .ok_or_else(|| {
                    Error::host_resource(
                        "§3.3: host limit: interface region exceeds host memory".into(),
                    )
                })?;
            let mut zero = Vec::new();
            zero.try_reserve_exact(n).map_err(|e| {
                Error::host_resource(format!("§3.3: host limit: interface region storage: {e}"))
            })?;
            zero.resize(n, 0);
            input.regions.insert(p.name.clone(), zero);
        }
    }
    if command == "run-oracle" {
        if exhaustive {
            return Err(fail("--exhaustive is a step-check option"));
        }
        let wide_input = OracleVectorInput {
            ins: vectors,
            args: input.args,
            regions: input.regions,

                ("code".into(), vector_number(&r.code)),

            ])
            .canonical()
        );
        return Ok(());

codex
I found two problems in the proposed wording. It requires F_B recounts for every region, although §5.3 gives actions no fault outcome. It also refers to existing independently validated exclusion classes, but ISA 0.9 only names the exclusion counters; it does not define their validation. The invocation-binding defect itself is real.

/bin/bash -lc 'python3 -I -B conformance/tools/ref_catalog.py --check' in /home/tobias/Projects/bennett-workspace/bennett-isa-spec
 succeeded in 650ms:
    35	If the region carries claims for both budgets, a flat `fault_rate` can
    36	mean either result. Neither the first claim nor the root invocation of
    37	an unrelated caller may be silently selected.
    38	
    39	| Alternative | Assessment |
    40	|---|---|
    41	| Inherit from an arbitrary claim | Ambiguous; changes when claims are reordered. |
    42	| Restrict every region to one claim/invocation | Removes existing certificate expressiveness. |
    43	| Bind each rate explicitly (recommended) | Recounts exactly one invocation/domain, with context keys where scheduled. |
    44	
    45	## Recommended exact spec diff
    46	
    47	Extend §12.3's existing standalone record to:
    48	
    49	```text
    50	fault_rate:{invocation:{args,budget},domain,faults_by_code,
    51	            undefined_excluded,layout_dependent_excluded}
    52	fault_rate_contexts?:[{context_key,invocation:{args,budget},domain,
    53	                      faults_by_code,undefined_excluded,
    54	                      layout_dependent_excluded}]
    55	```
    56	
    57	Append:
    58	
    59	```text
    60	Every fault_rate recount binds its own invocation by K13's exact typed
    61	args, budget, validation and encoding rules. It inherits no claim's
    62	invocation. Counts are recomputed from F_B on this invocation and the
    63	declared domain, with the existing independently validated exclusion
    64	classes reported separately; a nonzero incoming fault-register word is
    65	not a fault event. Counts are nonnegative exact integers and cannot
    66	exceed the enumerated applicable domain count.
    67	
    68	For a scheduled region reached in several §12.1 contexts, report exactly
    69	one fault_rate_contexts record for each such context, in canonical context
    70	enumeration order. Each explicit invocation MUST equal that context's
    71	resolved values. The legacy fault_rate record is the first context's
    72	record with context_key removed; it is a deterministic projection and
    73	discharges only that context. With one context the legacy record suffices
    74	and MUST bind that context. Standalone records have their explicit
    75	invocation and no implied context. Missing, duplicated or conflicting
    76	bindings reject the requested recount; old unbound rate data cannot
    77	discharge a verified fault-rate obligation.
    78	```
    79	
    80	A future arrays-only schema may remove the projection. This proposal
    81	retains the required old field while making its meaning deterministic.
    82	It does not change oracle fault semantics, exclusion classification or
    83	K13's distinction between token tuple and process-local id.
    84	
    85	## Hashes, catalog and consumers
    86	
    87	Certificates gain invocation/context bytes and change identity; the
    88	object/region closure, target, schedule and ref catalog do not change.
    89	Plugin and checker must bind the same table entries/actual token tuples.
    90	Rung gates must select the exact invocation for their zero-fault check.
    91	No resource or fault result for one budget covers a different budget by
    92	guessing; monotonic success can be a separate proved statement.
    93	
    94	## Conformance vectors and verification
    95	
    96	Use both 256-tuple budgets above, reordered claims, args=[], tokens with
    97	equal tuples/different ids, missing budget, conflicting scheduled context,
    98	and duplicate/omitted context records. Check all fault values, including
    99	an incoming word 1: budget-1 failure XORs it to 0 but is still code 1;
   100	budget-2 success leaves it 1 but is not a fault. Counts for undefined
  3854	### 12.3 Schema and composition
  3855	
  3856	```
  3857	  versions; bindings { object_hash, interface_hash, closure_hash (required), reference_program_hash, dialect_mapping_version, target_hash, schedule_hash?, invocation? (K13) }
  3858	  producer { name, version, materialisation { llvm_version, pipeline, normaliser_version } }
  3859	  regions { <closure_hash>: { kind, signature, bounds, estimate? (K12), frame (required typed-record descriptor, §9.4), resources { name: decimal }, gates_used { gate: { count, scheme?, epsilon? } },
  3860	            claims: [ <claim> … ], verification: { oracle: i, borrow_frames: [i], inverse_pairs: [ { g, ginv, claim: i, budget: [B,heap,stack,depth], arg_tokens?: { "<arg>": [B,heap,stack,depth] } } ], unitary: [i], channel: [i],
  3861	                                                   fault_rate: { domain, faults_by_code, undefined_excluded, layout_dependent_excluded } },
  3862	            cleanup: { "<variant>:<inherited_controls>": [ { path, realisation } ] },
  3863	            error:   { "<variant>:<inherited_controls>": { local: { "operator_norm"?: v, "diamond"?: v }, inclusive: { "operator_norm"?: v, "diamond"?: v } } },
  3864	            definedness_assumptions: [ids] } }
  3865	  composition { multiplicities: { "<hash>:<variant>:<inherited_controls>": n (from the realised schedule) }, epsilon_total: { "diamond": v }, method: "sum_over_multiplicities" }
  3866	```
  3867	
  3868	**Invocation binding (K13).** An invocation is
  3869	`{ args: [value, …], budget: [B,heap,stack,depth] }`, with every value
  3870	resolved before replay and all four budget components explicit even
  3871	when zero. `args` is in signature order: `iN` and `ptr` values are
  3872	canonical **unsigned decimal strings** of exactly that bit-vector
  3873	value (ptr has `ptr_width` bits); `f64` is `0x` followed by exactly 16
  3874	lowercase hex digits of its IEEE 754 binary64 bit pattern, most
  3875	significant nibble first (so −0 and NaN payloads are not lost); a token
  3876	is its four-component tuple of canonical nonnegative decimal strings,
  3877	not a process-local token id. Budget components are also canonical
  3878	nonnegative decimal strings. Signature types disambiguate encodings;
  3879	widths/ranges and every tuple MUST pass §5.4 validation. `params_hash`
  3880	is SHA-256 of the UTF-8 JCS **args array**, no trailing LF (§9.2), not
  3881	of the whole invocation. Thus equal token tuples have equal parameter
  3882	hashes regardless of ids, while +0 and −0 `f64` parameters differ.
  3883	
  3884	Each standalone table/program/structural claim MUST carry its own
  3885	`invocation`; it binds
  3886	**every** classical argument and budget. Missing values have no implicit
  3887	zero/default. Claims with no arguments still bind `args: []` and their
  3888	budget. K4 inverse-pair claims instead bind the entry's budget,
  3889	`arg_tokens` and quantified `args` domain as already specified; this
  3890	rule does not replace that quantification by one chosen argument.
  3891	A parameter-uniform structural claim MAY instead bind
  3892	`invocation: { args: "all", budget: "all" }`: its checker MUST discharge
  3893	the rule's side conditions for every well-typed argument vector and
  3894	valid budget, not by replaying a chosen instance. Thus generic library
  3895	and adjoint/translation proofs retain their universal scope.
  3896	A scheduled certificate MUST carry the root `bindings.invocation`;
  3897	its args hash and budget MUST equal the root schedule's bindings, and
  3898	all nested arguments and budgets MUST be resolved at each realised
  3899	invocation context (§12.1), including default-covered contexts.
  3900	Explicit nested bindings, if supplied, MUST equal those resolutions;
  3901	a root binding MUST NOT be silently reused for a different callee.
  3902	A checker MUST reject absent or conflicting bindings before replay,
  3903	and an executor may use a verified result only for the invocation(s)
  3904	it actually covers. Reference table/program/structural claims need no
  3905	schedule; they prove only their reference relation, not a target
  3906	realisation. Scheduled/numerical/channel claims retain the expansion
  1185	### 5.3 Quantum action
  1186	
  1187	**Semantics.** The body denotes a unitary U on mut ⊗ ins ⊗ mem ⊗
  1188	(borrowed), where mem includes both `mut` and `in` interface regions,
  1189	block-diagonal in the computational basis of `ins` and of `in`-region
  1190	cells, identity on borrowed and external reference systems (§5.6),
  1191	workspace returned clean, with domain D every value of each `mut`
  1192	and `in` bundle, every cell of every `mut` and `in` interface region and
  1193	every borrowed bundle, workspace initially at 0 and local regions at
  1194	image. [deps: ISA-5.1-P1, ISA-5.2-P4, ISA-3.3-P19, ISA-5.6-P1, ISA-14.2] No fault outcome. Coherent internal
  1195	control yields the padded unitary under declared bounds, part of the
  1196	action's identity. **Permutation action**: realised unitary a
  1197	permutation matrix (ε = 0, permutation gates only, transitively).
  1198

  1200	qstore qexchm`; `qpred qunpred qif qloop`; `oracle_call action_call`;
